For an ascending domain (e.g. `[0, 12]`) this is fine, but for a descending/reversed domain (e.g. `[12, 0]`, which happens with `reverse: true` or a left-oriented axis in this bug report) `span()` returns a negative number. Dividing by `minStep` and flooring then yields a negative tick count, which gets passed straight to d3-scale's `scale.ticks(count)`. D3 treats any non-positive count as \"produce nothing\" for positive counts, but for negative-but-not-below-`Math.min`-clamped counts it can still return a set of ticks with spacing smaller than intended, since the `Math.min(count, ...)` clamp is no longer bounding `count` correctly — effectively the `tickMinStep` constraint stops constraining anything once the domain is descending.\n\n**Fix:** wrap the span computation in `Math.abs()` so the min-step distance is always treated as a positive interval regardless of domain direction:\n\n```js\n// packages/vega-scale/src/ticks.js\nif (minStep != null) {\n  count = Math.min(\n    count,\n    Math.floor((Math.abs(span(scale.domain())) / minStep) || 1) + 1\n  );\n}\n```\n\nThis matches a precaution already taken elsewhere in the codebase (`axis.js` wraps `span(range(...))` in `abs()` for reversed ranges), just missing here.\n\nI also added a regression test in `packages/vega-scale/test/ticks-test.js` verifying `tickCount` produces the same clamped count for both `[0, 12]` and `[12, 0]` domains with `minStep = 3`.
